interventions, while making the target behavior move in the desired direction and keeping unrelated behavior stable.","importance":"The contribution is not a pretraining method, but it is relevant to model control, interpretability, and the interface between frozen base models and post-training interventions. It turns activation steering into a more explicit control problem: whether the target is reachable, whether the effect reverses with the sign of the intervention, whether the response is smooth under changing intervention strength, and whether off-target behavior is preserved. This is useful for studying refusal control, stylistic steering, factual behavior, and safety interventions. The main limitation is scope: it is an inference-time framework and does not explain how these controllable directions arise during pretraining.","technical_approach":"The method treats selected internal activations of an autoregressive Transformer as temporary control inputs. The model parameters remain fixed. A behavior is represented by a task-specific readout, such as a logit difference, a sentiment score, a refusal score, or a vector of multiple-choice logits. For each input, the desired target readout defines a displacement from the model’s original behavior. Sparse internal control then searches for a compact set of site-direction pairs whose interventions can produce that displacement under constraints on sparsity, off-target preservation, and intervention energy. The local target-controllability formulation estimates how candidate internal directions affect the behavioral readout. Driver-OMP selects drivers by a sparse reconstruction objective, while Coverage-Driver prioritizes prompt-level coverage. The evaluation protocol has six axes: reachability, signed reversal, dose response, feedback controllability, off-target preservation, and held-out reuse.","experiments":"The provided content describes the formulation, algorithms, and evaluation protocol, but does not include verifiable numerical results, model sizes, dataset sizes, or ablation numbers. The experimental design is clear: compare different intervention sites, direction rules, and sparse selectors under a shared six-axis control protocol. The most important empirical questions are likely the tradeoff between sparsity and reachability, whether Driver-OMP overfits target prompts compared with Coverage-Driver, and how intervention energy affects off-target behavior.","reading_guide":"Skim in 15–20 minutes. Focus on the problem formulation in the introduction, then read the sparse internal control section for the definitions of behavioral readouts, target displacement, candidate site-direction pairs, and constraints. The Driver-OMP and Coverage-Driver sections are the main technical parts. The six-axis evaluation protocol is the most reusable component; it can serve as a checklist for future activation steering experiments. Related work can be read lightly unless the goal is to connect the framework to mechanistic interpretability or model editing.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"读 formulation 和 selector 就够；若 off-target preservation 实验干净，可用于内部 steering ablation。","en":"Read the formulation and selectors; if the off-target preservation tests are clean, it is worth porting into steering ablations."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Xizhe Zhang"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["activation-steering","interpretability","inference-time-control","sparse-intervention"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04349","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.04349","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2611.03650","title":"Alignment Faking Depends on the Value, Not Just the Model","abstract":"Language models can follow a directive when they believe they are monitored and abandon it when they believe they are not, a pattern termed Alignment Faking. Prior work establishes this for harmful-content objectives and a handful of moral-foundations values, leaving open how it distributes across a wider value space. We argue it is value-dependent: whether compliance survives a change in perceived oversight is a property of the model--value pairing, not of the model alone. These are writer heads. The key distinction is between reading location and written content: a head may attend cleanly to the target image but fail to encode useful target-image information for later layers.
